```typescript
  47 │
→ 48 │       this.results.orderNo = this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(`<code>${results.data[0].orderId}</code>`)
  49 │       this.results.email = results.data[0].email
  50 │       this.results.totalPrice = results.data[0].totalPrice
```

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◕ [M-214](#m-214) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (🟠 [F-045](#f-045))

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-024"></a><a id="f-024"></a>
#### F-024 · GitHub Actions referenced by mutable tags or branches

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (3)

**Found by:** survey and scanners

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-042](#w-042) - Build pipeline trusts mutable third-party references

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-3](#tb-3) 🌐 External - external → CI/CD Pipeline · Authentication: The tb-3 assumption that every uses: reference is SHA-pinned fails here: a tag-only third-party action receives `GITHUB_TOKEN`.

**Instances (3):** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: 188; `.github/workflows/image_actions.yml`: 33; `.github/workflows/codeql-analysis.yml`: 23

**Issue:** Several workflows reference actions by tag or branch, and a tag or branch can be moved, so a compromised publisher can inject code into a reference that is already in use. At `.github/workflows/ci.yml:188`, coverallsapp/github-action@v2 runs in the coverage-report job on push to juice-shop.

That workflow has a least-privilege permissions block, and the action is GitHub first-party.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```yaml
.github/workflows/image_actions.yml
  31 │       - name: Compress Images
  32 │         id: calibre
→ 33 │         uses: calibreapp/image-actions@main
  34 │         with:
  35 │           githubToken: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-196](#m-196) — Pin image-actions and other actions to commit SHAs

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-SUPPLY-01](#tc-supply-01) · Rules: IAC-011

<a id="t-025"></a><a id="f-025"></a>
#### F-025 · Lockfile generation disabled by npm configuration

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.npmrc:1`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-042](#w-042) - Build pipeline trusts mutable third-party references

**Issue:** npmrc disables lockfile generation, so every npm install in CI, release and Docker builds resolves the caret ranges in `package.json` and `frontend/package.json` fresh from the registry. Install scripts are enabled in those runs.

A malicious version published inside an allowed range (for example through maintainer account takeover) is installed and runs its lifecycle scripts during the next build, with no lockfile diff to signal it.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```
→ 1 │ package-lock=false
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-197](#m-197) — Commit lockfiles and install with npm ci

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-1357](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1357.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

### 🟡 Medium (96)

<a id="t-085"></a><a id="f-085"></a>
#### F-085 · Hardcoded testing account credentials in client bundle

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Client Application  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:62`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-010](#w-010) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** At `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:62` LoginComponent holds a testing username and password as plain string literals in public properties. Any visitor who downloads the compiled Angular bundle can extract them and authenticate as that account if it exists on the server.

No control is in place.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `login.component.ts:61-62` defines `testingUsername` and `testingPassword`. `routes/login.ts:65` references the same credentials, but whether the account exists in the database was not inspected.

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ◑ [M-251](#m-251) — Remove embedded test credentials from the client

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03)

<a id="t-112"></a><a id="f-112"></a>
#### F-112 · TOTP shared secrets stored in cleartext

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Sequelize application data access  ·  **Location:** `models/user.ts:112`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-DATA-02](#tc-data-02))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-032](#w-032) - TOTP shared secrets stored in cleartext (`user.ts:112`)

**Issue:** At `models/user.ts:112`, the `totpSecret` column is a plain STRING with no setter or encryption. An attacker who can read the Users table gets each enrolled user's TOTP seed and can generate valid second-factor codes.

Combined with the `MD5` password hashes, a database leak exposes both authentication factors.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `totpSecret` at `models/user.ts:112-115` is stored as a plain string column next to the `MD5` password hash.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-276](#m-276) — Encrypt TOTP secrets with a managed key

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-312](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/312.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-02](#tc-data-02)

<a id="t-128"></a><a id="f-128"></a>
#### F-128 · Cookie signing secret hardcoded in server setup

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:289`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-010](#w-010) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** `cookieParser` is set up with a literal signing secret in `server.ts`. Anyone with access to the source can forge signed cookies that the server accepts as authentic.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ◑ [M-292](#m-292) — Load cookie signing secret from secret store

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03)

<a id="t-131"></a><a id="f-131"></a>
#### F-131 · Hardcoded wallet mnemonic exposes Ethereum private key

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/checkKeys.ts:10`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-010](#w-010) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** `routes/checkKeys.ts:10` contains a hardcoded BIP-39 mnemonic seed phrase as a string literal, and the private key is derived from it on every request. Anyone who can read the application source, or the shipped bundle according to the survey, gets the mnemonic.

Disclosure of the mnemonic gives full control of the derived Ethereum wallet and all assets it holds.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Line 10 assigns a literal 12-word mnemonic. Lines 11-12 derive `privateKey` from it, and line 18 compares `req.body.privateKey` against that key.

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ◑ [M-295](#m-295) — Move wallet key material to a secret store

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03)

<a id="t-138"></a><a id="f-138"></a>
#### F-138 · TOTP secrets stored in plaintext in the user table

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/2fa.ts:129`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-02](#tc-data-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-033](#w-033) - TOTP secrets stored in plaintext in the user table (`2fa.ts:129`)

**Issue:** Anyone who reads the SQLite database, for example through SQL injection in the login query or file access, obtains every user's TOTP seed and can generate valid second-factor codes.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-302](#m-302) — Stop storing sensitive data in cleartext (🟡 [F-138](#f-138))

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-312](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/312.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-02](#tc-data-02)

<a id="t-139"></a><a id="f-139"></a>
#### F-139 · HMAC key for security answers hardcoded in source

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:42`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-010](#w-010) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** An attacker with the source and a database dump uses the hardcoded HMAC key to run offline dictionary attacks on security answers, then resets account passwords.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ◑ [M-303](#m-303) — Move the HMAC key into managed secret storage

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03)

<a id="t-140"></a><a id="f-140"></a>
#### F-140 · Seeded account passwords embedded in the login handler

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/login.ts:60`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-010](#w-010) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** A reader of the source learns the cleartext passwords of the seeded support, test and other accounts and logs in as those users through POST `/rest/user/login`.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ◑ [M-304](#m-304) — Remove cleartext account passwords from source code

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03)

<a id="t-077"></a><a id="f-077"></a>
#### F-077 · Reset-password rate limit keyed on client-controlled IP

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:346`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-012](#w-012) - Reset-password rate limit keyed on client-controlled IP (`server.ts:346`)

**Issue:** `server.ts` enables 'trust proxy' and keys the reset-password limiter on headers['X-Forwarded-For'] or `req.ip`. Under trust proxy, `req.ip` is itself derived from the client-sent X-Forwarded-For header.

An attacker rotates that header on each request to get a fresh bucket of 100 attempts, then brute-forces security answers for password reset.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Apply rate limiting and lock-out thresholds on authentication endpoints → ◑ [M-243](#m-243) — Key reset rate limit on trusted client address

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-307](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-078"></a><a id="f-078"></a>
#### F-078 · Review author taken from request body

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/createProductReviews.ts:26`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Issue:** PUT `/rest/products/:id/reviews` has no auth guard (`server.ts:633`). It stores `req.body.author` as provided, so anyone can post reviews under any user's email. The forged reviews also feed the chatbot's tool output.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-244](#m-244) — Verify token signatures before trusting claims

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-345](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/345.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-079"></a><a id="f-079"></a>
#### F-079 · No password strength policy on registration or change

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:410`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** Registration only checks that the trimmed password is not empty, and password change only checks for an empty value and a matching repeat. Users can therefore set one-character or common passwords, which an attacker can guess by online brute force against login, which is not rate limited in the inspected routes.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Enforce a length and complexity policy and reject reused / breached passwords → ◑ [M-245](#m-245) — Enforce server-side password strength policy

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-521](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/521.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04)

<a id="t-080"></a><a id="f-080"></a>
#### F-080 · CAPTCHA answer disclosed and reusable

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/captcha.ts:31`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** GET `/rest/captcha` returns the captcha object, including its answer. `verifyCaptcha` never consumes a captcha after use. A bot reads the answer, or reuses one solved `captchaId`, and posts unlimited automated feedback that passes as human.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-246](#m-246) — Withhold CAPTCHA answers and make them single-use

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-804](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/804.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-081"></a><a id="f-081"></a>
#### F-081 · Wallet ownership accepted from request body

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/nftMint.ts:41`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-4](#tb-4) - external → Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface · Authentication: The internet-facing handler trusts a client-supplied `walletAddress` at this crossing with no authentication or proof of ownership.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker reads a legitimate NFTMinted event on the public Sepolia chain to learn a minter's address, then POSTs that address as `walletAddress` to `/rest/web3/walletNFTVerify`. The server checks only that the address is in the Set, so it credits the mint to the attacker without any proof of key ownership.

Attackers can claim credit for on-chain actions performed by other wallets, which corrupts challenge or score state.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `nftMint.ts:41-44` takes `walletAddress` from the body and solves the challenge if the address is in `addressesMinted`. `web3Wallet.ts:15-16` likewise trusts the claimed address.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-247](#m-247) — Require signed nonce proof of wallet ownership

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-290](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/290.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-083"></a><a id="f-083"></a>
#### F-083 · OAuth login trusts userinfo without checking token audience

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Client Application  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/Services/user.service.ts:68`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-06](#tc-authn-06))

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: `oauthLogin` only fetches userinfo with no audience check, but the code that uses the profile to sign the user in is outside the supplied source.

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Issue:** The client signs a user in from whatever profile Google returns for a supplied access token. It never checks that the token was issued to this `client_id`.

An access token obtained by another application for the victim can therefore be replayed through the #`access_token` callback to sign in as the victim.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** ◑ [M-174](#m-174) — Manual review: verify OAuth login trusts userinfo without checking token audience · ◑ [M-249](#m-249) — Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification (🟡 [F-083](#f-083))

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-345](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/345.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-06](#tc-authn-06)

<a id="t-086"></a><a id="f-086"></a>
#### F-086 · Token checks omit issuer and audience, and the jws path

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:55`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-03](#tc-authn-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Issue:** An attacker who captures an expired 2FA `tmpToken` or `setupToken` can replay it at any time, because `jws.verify` checks no exp. Session, second-factor and setup tokens are signed with the same key and carry no audience, so a second-factor token also passes the `isAuthorized` session gate.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `jws.verify` at line 55 checks only the signature, and its decoded payload is trusted at `routes/2fa.ts:20`. Lines 52 and 189 configure no issuer or audience.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-252](#m-252) — Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification (🟡 [F-086](#f-086))

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-345](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/345.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-03](#tc-authn-03)

<a id="t-087"></a><a id="f-087"></a>
#### F-087 · No minimum password strength on reset

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:26`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** Users can set one-character or common passwords through password reset or registration. Accounts with such passwords are easy to take over by guessing or credential spraying.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Reset checks only that the new password is not empty and matches its repeat (lines 26 and 30). The registration pre-check at `server.ts:410` tests only for a non-zero length.

**Fix:** Enforce a length and complexity policy and reject reused / breached passwords → ◑ [M-253](#m-253) — Enforce a server-side password strength policy

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-521](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/521.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04)

<a id="t-088"></a><a id="f-088"></a>
#### F-088 · Session map lookup ignores token expiry and revocation

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:178`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-2](#tb-2) 🌐 External - external → Authentication &amp; Session Surface · Authentication: Card and address routes rely on `appendUserId` map lookup instead of `isAuthorized()` JWT verification at the internet boundary.

**Issue:** `appendUserId` resolves the caller's identity from the in-memory `tokenMap` by raw token without verifying signature freshness; a stolen token keeps working on `/api/Cards` and `/api/Addresss` after its 6h exp because entries are never removed.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-254](#m-254) — Verify JWT and expiry before trusting session map

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-613](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/613.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-090"></a><a id="f-090"></a>
#### F-090 · Missing range validation on basket item quantity

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Sequelize application data access  ·  **Location:** `models/basketitem.ts:39`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** A shopper submits a basket item with a zero or negative quantity; the BasketItem model accepts any integer, so unless the route rejects it the negative line reduces the order total or distorts stock accounting.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `BasketItem.quantity` is declared as `DataTypes.INTEGER` without validate { min: 1 }, unlike `Hint.order` which enforces min: 1; route-level checks were not in scope.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-256](#m-256) — Add min 1 integer validation to BasketItem quantity

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-1284](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1284.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-098"></a><a id="f-098"></a>
#### F-098 · Fetched profile image stored without type

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:30`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-FILE-02](#tc-file-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-039](#w-039) - Fetched profile image stored without type or size check

**Issue:** A logged-in user points `imageUrl` at an attacker host that returns an SVG with script, or a very large body, using a .svg URL. The server streams the body unchecked into the public uploads directory as an .svg file. This hosts active content on the shop origin or exhausts disk space.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Validate uploaded file type, size, and storage path; never execute uploaded content → ◑ [M-262](#m-262) — Validate fetched image content type and size

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-434](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/434.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/) · Verification items: [TC-FILE-02](#tc-file-02)

<a id="t-099"></a><a id="f-099"></a>
#### F-099 · Campaign coupon validity trusts client-supplied date

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/order.ts:201`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** At checkout a user sends `couponData` as base64 'WMNSDY2019-&lt;`validOn`&gt;' where `validOn` is the published campaign timestamp; the server compares the client-supplied date to the campaign date instead of the current time, so expired campaign discounts (up to 75%) always apply.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-263](#m-263) — Use trusted client IPs for rate limiting

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-807](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/807.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-100"></a><a id="f-100"></a>
#### F-100 · Basket item update accepts client-supplied BasketId

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/basketItems.ts:70`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Privileged attributes lack an enforced write allowlist

**Issue:** PUT `/api/BasketItems/:id` passes the request body on to the generated update. The hook notices a body BasketId that differs from the caller's basket but only records a challenge and continues, so a caller can move an item into another customer's basket.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `basketItems.ts:70` compares `req.body.BasketId` with `user.bid` only for challenge solving, then `next()` runs at line 77. BasketId is a writable model attribute.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-264](#m-264) — Allow-list updatable basket item fields

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03)

<a id="t-101"></a><a id="f-101"></a>
#### F-101 · Feedback can be created in another user's name

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:406`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** A caller posts to `/api/Feedbacks` with a JSON body that sets UserId to another user's id. The finale create stores that UserId as the author. The `forgedFeedbackChallenge` middleware only detects the mismatch and calls `next()`, so the feedback is attributed to the victim.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/verify.ts:33` compares body UserId with the caller only to solve a challenge. `models/feedback.ts:31` defines UserId as a writable column. `server.ts:486` exposes Feedback through finale without excluded attributes.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-265](#m-265) — Derive feedback owner from the authenticated session

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03)

<a id="t-102"></a><a id="f-102"></a>
#### F-102 · Review update lacks ownership check

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/updateProductReviews.ts:18`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-INPUT-02](#tc-input-02))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Issue:** An authenticated user sends PATCH `/rest/products/reviews` with a body such as `{"id":{"$ne":-1},"message":"x"}`. At `routes/updateProductReviews.ts:18` the body id is used as the update selector with no type check, no author filter and `multi:true`.

The user is looked up but is not used in the query.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Replace string concatenation in query operators with parameter binding → ◑ [M-266](#m-266) — Use parameterized database queries (🟡 [F-102](#f-102))

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-02](#tc-input-02)

<a id="t-103"></a><a id="f-103"></a>
#### F-103 · Stored username and reviews enter model context as instructions

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - verified attack-chain keystone in AC-T-007 (Privileged Action via Prompt Injection into a Tool-Calling Model); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-019](#w-019) - Stored username and reviews enter model context as instructions (`chat.ts:83`)

**Instances (2):** `routes/chat.ts`: 83, 149

**Issue:** LLM01/ASI01: `buildSystemPrompt` interpolates the stored username into the system prompt. The `getProductReviews` tool returns user-authored review text, and `searchProducts` returns product descriptions, to the model as tool results.

A user who sets an instruction-like username or posts such a review can steer later model runs, including other customers' sessions through the reviews.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-267](#m-267) — Isolate stored user text from model instructions

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-1427](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1427.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-111"></a><a id="f-111"></a>
#### F-111 · Client-side coupon validation trusts browser-supplied

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Client Application  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/payment/payment.component.ts:160`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-022](#w-022) - Client-side coupon validation trusts browser-supplied date

**Issue:** An attacker reads the expired campaign coupon codes and their validity dates from the bundle. They change the browser clock, or edit `sessionStorage` `couponDetails` directly, so that the client-computed date equals the campaign's `validOn` value.

Whether the server re-validates that date was not examined.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `payment.component.ts` embeds the campaign coupon table (lines 84-94). It stores `${coupon}-${clientDate}` in `sessionStorage` (line 160) and compares `clientDate` with `campaign.validOn` on the client (line 163). `order-summary.component.ts` line 79 sends `btoa(couponDetails)` to checkout.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-275](#m-275) — Enforce authorization on the server

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-602](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/602.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-114"></a><a id="f-114"></a>
#### F-114 · Null byte bypass of file type allowlist

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/fileServer.ts:28`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: Allowlist check precedes `cutOffPoisonNullByte`, but the helper's implementation is not in the supplied source, so truncation behavior cannot be confirmed.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker requests `/ftp/package.json.bak%2500.md`. The decoded parameter ends in '.md' and passes the allowlist, then `cutOffPoisonNullByte` truncates at '%00' and the server sends `package.json.bak` or other disallowed backups.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** ◑ [M-175](#m-175) — Manual review: verify Null byte bypass of file type allowlist · ◑ [M-278](#m-278) — Reject null bytes before extension validation

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-158](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/158.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-115"></a><a id="f-115"></a>
#### F-115 · CAPTCHA endpoint returns the expected answer

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/captcha.ts:31`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** An anonymous caller requests GET `/rest/captcha` and reads the answer field. `verifyCaptcha` checks feedback submissions against that same answer, so automated feedback spam bypasses the CAPTCHA.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◑ [M-279](#m-279) — Omit the CAPTCHA answer from the response

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-116"></a><a id="f-116"></a>
#### F-116 · Image CAPTCHA endpoint returns the expected answer

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/imageCaptcha.ts:31`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** A logged-in user requests GET `/rest/image-captcha` and reads the answer text. `verifyImageCaptcha` checks data-export requests against that answer, so the CAPTCHA can be solved automatically.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◑ [M-280](#m-280) — Stop exposing internal information to clients (🟡 [F-116](#f-116))

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-117"></a><a id="f-117"></a>
#### F-117 · User list exposes emails, login IPs and deluxe tokens of all

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:484`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-028](#w-028) - User list exposes emails, login IPs and deluxe tokens of all users

**Issue:** Any user can register through the open POST `/api/Users` and then call GET `/api/Users` with their JWT. The generated finale resource removes only password and `totpSecret`, so the response holds every account's email, role, `deluxeToken`, `lastLoginIp` and `profileImage`.

Every authenticated customer can see all users' email addresses, roles, last login IPs and deluxe tokens. These records support account targeting, credential stuffing and admin enumeration.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:484` configures the User resource with exclude ['password','totpSecret'] only. `models/user.ts:100` and :104 define `deluxeToken` and `lastLoginIp`, which stay in the serialized record. The list has no owner or role filter.

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◑ [M-281](#m-281) — Restrict user list to admins and allow-list fields

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-118"></a><a id="f-118"></a>
#### F-118 · Complaint list returns every user's complaint messages to any

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:489`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-029](#w-029) - Complaint list returns every user's complaint messages to any customer

**Issue:** An attacker with any customer account calls GET `/api/Complaints`. The generated finale list has no exclusions and no owner filter, so it returns all users' complaint messages, uploaded file names and UserIds.

Complaint text that customers write for support staff, which may contain personal or order details, can be read by every other customer.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:489` registers Complaint with exclude []. `models/complaint.ts:36` stores up to 4096 characters of free-text message per user. `server.ts:381` adds only authentication, with no ownership scoping.

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◑ [M-282](#m-282) — Stop exposing internal information to clients (🟡 [F-118](#f-118))

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-119"></a><a id="f-119"></a>
#### F-119 · Unauthenticated metrics endpoint exposes internal runtime state

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:729`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** Any anonymous caller requests GET `/metrics` and receives the full Prometheus register: process and runtime metrics, version info and challenge progress. This helps an attacker with reconnaissance.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◑ [M-283](#m-283) — Restrict metrics endpoint to monitoring clients

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-120"></a><a id="f-120"></a>
#### F-120 · Unauthenticated security question lookup enables

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/securityQuestion.ts:23`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHN-05](#tc-authn-05), [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Issue:** An anonymous caller requests GET `/rest/user/security-question?email=``&lt;victim&gt;`, which `routes/securityQuestion.ts:23` handles. The route is registered without a guard (`server.ts:599`).

An attacker can use this to list which accounts exist and learn each account's recovery question, which narrows reset-password guessing attacks.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-284](#m-284) — Return uniform responses for unknown accounts

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-204](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/204.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-05](#tc-authn-05), [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-121"></a><a id="f-121"></a>
#### F-121 · Whoami returns arbitrary user fields and supports

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/currentUser.ts:31`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** The fields parameter copies any attribute of the cached user record into the response, including the password hash. The callback parameter returns JSONP, so a third-party page that includes the URL as a script can read the victim's data wherever the cookie is sent.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-285](#m-285) — Allow-list whoami fields and remove JSONP

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-213](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/213.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-122"></a><a id="f-122"></a>
#### F-122 · Confidential discount rule embedded in extractable

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:105`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AI-05](#tc-ai-05))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Issue:** At `routes/chat.ts:105`, the system prompt carries a confidential, internal-only rule that grants a 15% courtesy discount on escalation. Any caller can use client-controlled messages to ask the chatbot to repeat its instructions or pose as an escalating complainant.

Internal business policy is disclosed, and attackers can use it to obtain unauthorized discounts.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `buildSystemPrompt` embeds a 'CONFIDENTIAL - INTERNAL ONLY' business rule, passed as system at `chat.ts:205` on an unauthenticated route.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-286](#m-286) — Remove confidential policy from the model system prompt

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-497](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/497.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-AI-05](#tc-ai-05)

<a id="t-123"></a><a id="f-123"></a>
#### F-123 · Server log files served to unauthenticated callers

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:283`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-05](#tc-data-05))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-035](#w-035) - Server log files served to unauthenticated callers (`server.ts:283`)

**Issue:** Any anonymous caller can browse `/support/logs` and download log files. These can contain client IPs and request URLs, including passwords sent in change-password query strings.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-287](#m-287) — Remove public access to log directory

**Classification:** Unauthenticated Management Plane · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-538](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/538.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-05](#tc-data-05)

<a id="t-124"></a><a id="f-124"></a>
#### F-124 · Encryption key directory publicly listed and served

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/keyServer.ts:14`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-040](#w-040) - Encryption key directory publicly listed and served (`keyServer.ts:14`)

**Issue:** At `routes/keyServer.ts:14` the `/encryptionkeys` directory is listed, and its files are served through `serveKeyFiles` at `/encryptionkeys/:file`. Any unauthenticated client can browse the listing and download any file in it.

Neither part inspected the directory contents, so it is not shown whether non-public keys are present.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-288](#m-288) — Serve only public keys from a dedicated path

**Classification:** Unauthenticated Management Plane · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-552](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/552.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01)

<a id="t-125"></a><a id="f-125"></a>
#### F-125 · Server access logs listed and served

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/logfileServer.ts:14`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker browses `/support/logs`, where `serveIndex` lists the log files. The attacker then downloads access logs through `/support/logs/:file`, which is served by `serveLogFiles`. The only check is that forward slashes are rejected in the file name.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-289](#m-289) — Remove public log routes or require admin auth

**Classification:** Unauthenticated Management Plane · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-552](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/552.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01)

<a id="t-126"></a><a id="f-126"></a>
#### F-126 · FTP file type allow-list checked before null byte cut

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/fileServer.ts:28`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker requests `/ftp/package.json.bak%2500.md`. The name passes the .md extension check, then the null byte and suffix are cut off, and the server sends the non-allow-listed backup file.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-290](#m-290) — Validate the final file name after normalization

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-626](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/626.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/) · Verification items: [TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01)

<a id="t-127"></a><a id="f-127"></a>
#### F-127 · Orders bound to the caller only by vowel-masked email

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Instances (2):** `routes/dataExport.ts`: 33; `routes/orderHistory.ts`: 17

**Issue:** Orders are stored and looked up by the email with vowels replaced by '*'. `routes/dataExport.ts:33` selects orders by the caller's masked email, and `routes/orderHistory.ts:17` binds order history the same way.

The caller is authenticated through the bearer token map, but order ownership depends only on the masked email.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-291](#m-291) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (🟡 [F-127](#f-127))

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01)

<a id="t-129"></a><a id="f-129"></a>
#### F-129 · Unauthenticated access to recycle requests by id

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/recycles.ts:14`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** GET `/api/Recycles/:id` is registered with no `isAuthorized` guard. It `JSON.parses` the path id into the where clause, so an anonymous caller can request `/api/Recycles/[1,2,3,...]` and read every user's recycle records (UserId, address and pickup data) through an IN query.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-293](#m-293) — Require auth and scope recycle lookup to caller

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-130"></a><a id="f-130"></a>
#### F-130 · Authenticated users can read all user and complaint records

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:363`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** A customer calls GET `/api/Users` with their own token. GET `/api/Users` and GET `/api/Users/:id` (`server.ts:363`, lines 363-365) are guarded only by `isAuthorized`, the JWT check.

The survey also reports that GET `/api/Complaints` (line 381) is guarded only by authentication, and finale lists all complaints without filtering on the caller's UserId.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-294](#m-294) — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint (🟡 [F-130](#f-130))

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-132"></a><a id="f-132"></a>
#### F-132 · Unauthenticated WebSocket Channel

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Real-time WebSocket Channel  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (3)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-011](#w-011) - Endpoints are reachable without enforced authentication

**Instances (3):** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts`: 30, 50, 36

**Issue:** An attacker opens a socket\.io connection with a non-browser client, so the CORS origin setting does not apply. The connection handler then sends every stored notification to the attacker. New solves are also broadcast to every socket with `io.emit`.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `io.on('connection')` at line 23 has no auth check. Lines 29-30 emit every notification, and `challengeUtils.ts:62-66` builds each notification with its flag.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-296](#m-296) — Require authentication on every exposed endpoint

**Classification:** Unauthenticated Management Plane · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-133"></a><a id="f-133"></a>
#### F-133 · Primary data store kept as an unencrypted SQLite file

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-07](#c-07) - SQLite Embedded Database  ·  **Location:** `models/index.ts:41`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-030](#w-030) - Primary data store kept as an unencrypted SQLite file (`index.ts:41`)

**Issue:** Anyone who can read data/juiceshop.sqlite can copy and open the whole database: user records, password hashes, TOTP secrets, cards and addresses. That includes someone with filesystem access, a backup, a container image layer or a path-traversal or file-serving flaw elsewhere.

Copying the file exposes all confidential and restricted application data at once.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `createSequelize` sets storage to 'data/juiceshop.sqlite' with dialect sqlite. No encryption (for example SQLCipher) or key option is configured.

**Fix:** Encrypt the data in transit and at rest with vetted primitives → ◑ [M-297](#m-297) — Encrypt the SQLite file at rest and restrict its access

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-311](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/311.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-134"></a><a id="f-134"></a>
#### F-134 · Password change sends passwords in GET query parameters

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Client Application  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/Services/user.service.ts:54`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHN-07](#tc-authn-07), [TC-DATA-04](#tc-data-04))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-037](#w-037) - Password change sends passwords in GET query parameters (`user.service.ts:54`)

**Issue:** At `frontend/src/app/Services/user.service.ts:54`, `changePassword` sends the current and new passwords as URL query parameters on a GET request to `/rest/user/change-password`. They end up in server access logs, proxy logs, browser history and possibly referrers. No control is in place.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `user.service.ts:54-55` concatenates `passwords.current`, new and repeat into a GET URL without encoding.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-298](#m-298) — Send password changes in a POST request body

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-598](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/598.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-07](#tc-authn-07), [TC-DATA-04](#tc-data-04)

<a id="t-135"></a><a id="f-135"></a>
#### F-135 · Issued JWT embeds password hash and TOTP secret

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/login.ts:24`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-05](#tc-data-05))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** Login places the `SELECT * user row in the JWT payload, which is signed but not encrypted. Anyone who gets the token from browser` storage, the non-HttpOnly cookie or logs can base64-decode the password hash and the TOTP secret.

Anyone who gets the token from browser storage, the non-HttpOnly cookie or logs can base64-decode the password hash and the TOTP secret.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-299](#m-299) — Limit JWT claims to a user id and role

**Classification:** Insecure Client-Side Storage · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-201](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/201.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-05](#tc-data-05)

<a id="t-136"></a><a id="f-136"></a>
#### F-136 · Password reset response returns full user record

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:46`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** A caller who supplies an email and the right security answer to POST `/rest/user/reset-password` receives the whole updated User row. The row includes the password hash, the `totpSecret` (which defeats 2FA) and the `deluxeToken`.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-300](#m-300) — Return a minimal status from the password reset endpoint

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-213](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/213.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-137"></a><a id="f-137"></a>
#### F-137 · Save login IP response returns full user record

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/saveLoginIp.ts:33`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** A logged-in user, or a script running in their session, calls GET `/rest/saveLoginIp`. The response contains the full User model, including the `MD5` password hash, the TOTP secret and the `deluxeToken`.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-301](#m-301) — Return only the updated login IP from saveLoginIp

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-213](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/213.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-141"></a><a id="f-141"></a>
#### F-141 · Session token cookie set without HttpOnly or Secure

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:192`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-027](#w-027) - Sensitive cookies remain accessible to browser scripts

**Issue:** `updateAuthenticatedUsers` sets the JWT in a cookie with default options, so any XSS in the SPA can read `document.cookie` and exfiltrate the session token, and it may be sent over plain HTTP.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Set `HttpOnly` on every session cookie → ◑ [M-305](#m-305) — Set secure session-cookie attributes

**Classification:** Insecure Client-Side Storage · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-1004](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1004.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-142"></a><a id="f-142"></a>
#### F-142 · Synchronous sandboxed evaluation of client-supplied

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/b2bOrder.ts:23`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: Sync eval with 2s timeout is shown, but it runs only if rce challenges are enabled; that runtime config is not shown.

**Weakness:** [W-014](#w-014) - Synchronous sandboxed evaluation of client-supplied order code (`b2bOrder.ts:23`)

**Issue:** POST `/b2b/v2/orders` evaluates `body.orderLinesData` with notevil inside `vm.runInContext`, which can block the event loop for up to 2 s. Any registered user (`isAuthorized` at `server.ts:424`) can repeatedly send looping expressions and stall the whole Node process.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◑ [M-176](#m-176) — Manual review: verify Synchronous sandboxed evaluation of client-supplied order code · ◑ [M-306](#m-306) — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-143"></a><a id="f-143"></a>
#### F-143 · Unbounded LLM consumption on unauthenticated chat endpoint

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:203`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-015](#w-015) - Unbounded LLM consumption on unauthenticated chat endpoint (`chat.ts:203`)

**Issue:** LLM10: POST `/rest/chat` is registered with no auth or rate limit, accepts a messages array of any length and calls `streamText` with no `maxOutputTokens`. Tool loops are capped at 10 steps, and provider retries come from config.

An attacker floods the endpoint with large histories, exhausting the paid provider budget and the server's stream capacity.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◑ [M-307](#m-307) — Rate-limit chat and cap tokens and history size

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-770](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/770.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-144"></a><a id="f-144"></a>
#### F-144 · Unbounded unauthenticated LLM consumption on chat endpoint

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:203`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: No route limiter or `maxOutputTokens` seen, but global rate limiting and body-size limits elsewhere in `server.ts` were not inspected; exploitation unproven.

**Weakness:** [W-016](#w-016) - Unbounded unauthenticated LLM consumption on chat endpoint (`chat.ts:203`)

**Issue:** LLM10. POST `/rest/chat` has no authentication or rate limiter and is registered at `server.ts:638`. Anyone can drive provider cost and server load.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◑ [M-177](#m-177) — Manual review: verify Unbounded unauthenticated LLM consumption on chat endpoint · ◑ [M-308](#m-308) — Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts (🟡 [F-144](#f-144))

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-770](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/770.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-145"></a><a id="f-145"></a>
#### F-145 · Unbounded in-memory code-fix cache keyed by request

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/vulnCodeFixes.ts:40`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** GET `/snippets/fixes/:key` and POST `/snippets/fixes` need no authentication. They pass the key from the request to `readFixes`, which runs `readdirSync` and then stores an entry for every distinct key in the module-level CodeFixes object (line 40), even when no fix exists.

An attacker can send many unique, long keys to grow heap memory and cause disk I/O until the process degrades.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◑ [M-309](#m-309) — Cache only known challenge keys

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-770](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/770.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-146"></a><a id="f-146"></a>
#### F-146 · Unbounded global wallet set filled

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/web3Wallet.ts:16`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** POST `/rest/web3/walletExploitAddress` needs no authentication. It adds `req.body.walletAddress`, an arbitrary value, to the module-level `walletsConnected` Set (line 16). An attacker can post many unique, large values to grow process memory without limit.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◑ [M-310](#m-310) — Validate addresses and bound the connected-wallet set

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-770](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/770.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-147"></a><a id="f-147"></a>
#### F-147 · Memory image upload unbounded and written before authentication

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:698`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-FILE-02](#tc-file-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** The disk upload handler for `/rest/memories` has no file size limit and runs before `appendUserId` checks the caller. Anyone can therefore write arbitrarily large files into the public images directory and exhaust disk space.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◑ [M-311](#m-311) — Limit memory upload size and authenticate before storing

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-770](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/770.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/) · Verification items: [TC-FILE-02](#tc-file-02)

<a id="t-148"></a><a id="f-148"></a>
#### F-148 · Unbounded entity and alias expansion on public upload

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/fileUpload.ts:109`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-017](#w-017) - Unbounded entity and alias expansion on public upload (`fileUpload.ts:109`)

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker repeatedly uploads billion-laughs XML or YAML alias bombs to `/file-upload`. Expansion is bounded only by a 2s vm timeout, not memory, so repeated requests consume CPU and can exhaust heap.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-312](#m-312) — Bound parser resource consumption

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-776](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/776.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-149"></a><a id="f-149"></a>
#### F-149 · Unanchored regex on untrusted socket data

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Real-time WebSocket Channel  ·  **Location:** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:46`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An attacker sends `verifySvgInjectionChallenge` with a very long string, up to socket\.io's message buffer limit, that never matches. The unanchored pattern starts with .* and runs on that string with no length cap.

Backtracking grows with the square of the input length or worse, which can block the single Node event loop for every user.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `data?.match(/.*\.\.\/\.\.\/\.\.[\w/-]*?\/redirect.../)` runs on unbounded, unauthenticated input. The actual backtracking cost and the configured buffer size were not measured.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-313](#m-313) — Bound input length and anchor the verification regex

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-1333](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1333.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-153"></a><a id="f-153"></a>
#### F-153 · Basket item ownership check bypassed with duplicate

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/basketItems.ts:42`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** `addBasketItem` checks `basketIds[0]` against the user's bid but saves `basketIds[basketIds.length-1]`. Posting a raw JSON body with two BasketId keys passes the check with the attacker's own id and adds items to a victim's basket.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-315](#m-315) — Derive BasketId from the authenticated session

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-154"></a><a id="f-154"></a>
#### F-154 · Product review author taken from request

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - verified attack-chain keystone in MODEL-AC-002 (Review authored or edited in another user's name); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/createProductReviews.ts:26`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An unauthenticated caller sends PUT `/rest/products/1/reviews` with body `{"author":"admin@juice-sh.op","message":"..."}`. No guard runs before the handler, and the handler stores the review with the author taken from the body, so the review appears to come from the victim.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-316](#m-316) — Bind review author to the authenticated user

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03)

<a id="t-155"></a><a id="f-155"></a>
#### F-155 · Data export returns orders matched by vowel-masked

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/dataExport.ts:33`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: Query uses vowel-masked email, but how orders are stored (masked email field) is in unserved order code, so the collision path is not fully shown.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An attacker registers an account whose email differs from the victim's only in vowels, for example 'elice@`x.io`' against 'alice@`x.io`'. POST `/rest/user/data-export` queries orders by the caller's email with vowels replaced by '*', so the export includes the victim's orders (products, prices, ETA).

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-178](#m-178) — Manual review: verify Data export returns orders matched by vowel-masked email · ◑ [M-317](#m-317) — Query exported orders by a unique caller identifier

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01)

<a id="t-156"></a><a id="f-156"></a>
#### F-156 · Data export selects orders by a masked non-unique

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/dataExport.ts:33`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An attacker registers an email that masks to the same value as a victim's (e.g. jam@... for jim@...). POST `/rest/user/data-export` queries orders by the vowel-masked email and returns the victim's orders in the attacker's export.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-318](#m-318) — Bind exported orders to the caller's user identifier

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01)

<a id="t-157"></a><a id="f-157"></a>
#### F-157 · Checkout accepts and empties another customer's basket

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/order.ts:35`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An authenticated customer posts to `/rest/basket/`&lt;victim id&gt;`/checkout`. `placeOrder` loads the victim's basket by id, creates an order from its products under the caller's e-mail, and deletes the victim's basket items.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `BasketModel.findOne({ where: { id } })` uses the path id. `BasketItemModel.destroy({ where: { BasketId: id } })` at `order.ts:51` clears the victim's items.

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-319](#m-319) — Restrict checkout to the caller's own basket

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01)

<a id="t-158"></a><a id="f-158"></a>
#### F-158 · Order history lists orders of all users sharing

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/orderHistory.ts:17`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An attacker registers an email whose vowel-masked form equals a victim's. GET `/rest/order-history` filters orders only by that masked string and returns the victim's orders to the attacker.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-320](#m-320) — Scope order history by the caller's user identifier

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-159"></a><a id="f-159"></a>
#### F-159 · Order tracking returns any order without caller

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/trackOrder.ts:18`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An unauthenticated caller requests GET `/rest/track-order/:id` with another customer's order id and receives the stored order document, since no authentication or owner check runs on the route.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-321](#m-321) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (🟡 [F-159](#f-159))
