<a id="t-160"></a><a id="f-160"></a>
#### F-160 · Basket items readable and deletable across users by ID

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:359`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** A logged-in user enumerates `/api/BasketItems/:id` with GET and DELETE. The generated handlers run after `isAuthorized` only, so the user reads and removes items in other customers' baskets.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - BasketItem carries BasketId, but the GET and DELETE generated routes do not compare it with the caller's basket.

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-322](#m-322) — Scope basket item access to the caller's basket

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01)

<a id="t-161"></a><a id="f-161"></a>
#### F-161 · Any authenticated user can delete any feedback entry

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:361`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** A logged-in customer sends DELETE `/api/Feedbacks/:id` for other users' feedback IDs. Only authentication is checked, so the customer deletes feedback they do not own, a moderation function meant for administrators.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Feedbacks/:id has only `isAuthorized`; the Feedback model stores UserId, but no ownership or role comparison occurs before finale deletes the row.

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-323](#m-323) — Check owner or admin role before deleting feedback

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-162"></a><a id="f-162"></a>
#### F-162 · Any authenticated user can read other users' account records

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:365`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** A logged-in customer sends GET `/api/Users/``&lt;n&gt;` with their own valid JWT and iterates n. The finale-generated read returns each user's email, role, `deluxeToken`, `lastLoginIp` and `profileImage`. No check binds :id to the caller.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:365` registers only `isAuthorized` on GET `/api/Users/:id`. The finale resource at 501-503 serves the User model; its only exclusions are password and `totpSecret` (484). The User resource has no read hook.

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-324](#m-324) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (🟡 [F-162](#f-162))

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01)

<a id="t-163"></a><a id="f-163"></a>
#### F-163 · Unauthenticated read of any recycle record by id

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:388`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** Any caller, even without logging in, requests GET `/api/Recycles/``&lt;id&gt;`, or an array id such as [1,2,3] that `JSON.parse` accepts. They receive other users' recycle records, including UserId, AddressId, quantity and pickup date.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:388` registers `getRecycleItem` with no guard. `routes/recycles.ts:14` queries only by the id parsed from the path and never filters on the caller's UserId.

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-325](#m-325) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (🟡 [F-163](#f-163))

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01)

<a id="t-164"></a><a id="f-164"></a>
#### F-164 · Unauthenticated creation of security answers for arbitrary

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:501`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker POSTs to `/api/SecurityAnswers` with the UserId of a victim who has no security answer yet and an answer the attacker chooses. The attacker then uses the password-reset flow with that answer to take over the account.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - The finale resource for SecurityAnswer is registered with no guard on POST, and the model accepts UserId from the request body.

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-326](#m-326) — Bind security answer creation to the authenticated user

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03)

<a id="t-165"></a><a id="f-165"></a>
#### F-165 · Deluxe membership granted without payment verification

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/deluxe.ts:43`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An authenticated customer POSTs `/rest/deluxe-membership` with `paymentMode` set to any value other than 'wallet' or 'card' (or with no wallet row). Neither payment branch runs, and `upgradeToDeluxe` updates the user role to deluxe and issues a new token, granting deluxe pricing for free.

The wallet check-then-decrement is also non-atomic.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-327](#m-327) — Require a successful payment before role upgrade

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-841](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/841.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-166"></a><a id="f-166"></a>
#### F-166 · Any authenticated user can create catalog products

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:369`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** A registered customer sends POST `/api/Products` with a valid token and sets any name, price, `deluxePrice` and description. The finale-generated create handler stores the product, so the customer manipulates the shop catalog, a task meant for administrators.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:369` guards the route only with `isAuthorized`, which is a JWT signature check. The generated Product resource (`server.ts:485`, 501) performs the create, and no role check is registered.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-328](#m-328) — Restrict product writes to an admin role check

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-167"></a><a id="f-167"></a>
#### F-167 · Any authenticated user can list all users' complaints

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:381`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** A customer calls GET `/api/Complaints` and receives every complaint of every user, including message text and uploaded file names, because the generated list has no role check and no UserId scope.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:381` guards the route only with `isAuthorized`. The Complaint finale resource (`server.ts:489`, 501) has no list hook among the lines read, so it returns all rows.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-329](#m-329) — Scope complaint listing to the caller or admins

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-168"></a><a id="f-168"></a>
#### F-168 · Deluxe role granted without payment for unknown

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/deluxe.ts:43`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An authenticated customer sends POST `/rest/deluxe-membership` with `paymentMode` set to any value other than 'wallet' or 'card'. Both payment checks are skipped, and the handler updates the user's role to deluxe and returns a new deluxe token.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-330](#m-330) — Deny deluxe upgrade unless a payment succeeds

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-863](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/863.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-169"></a><a id="f-169"></a>
#### F-169 · Owner field UserId accepted from the request body on generated

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - elevated to High because verified finding evidence ties the weakness to confirmed internet ingress tb-1  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:382`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Privileged attributes lack an enforced write allowlist

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → Juice Shop Express API · Validation: The finding's evidence line is the interface this boundary cites; CWE-915 breaks its validation condition.<br>_⬆ Confirmed internet ingress at tb-1 raised this finding: Medium → High._

**Issue:** At `server.ts:382`, POST `/api/Complaints` runs only `isAuthorized`, which checks the JWT signature, and then finale creates the record. Basket items and cards call `appendUserId`, but this route does not, and nothing ties UserId to the caller.

On those routes a user could also file deletion requests in the victim's name, or attach a recycling pickup to the victim's address by sending the victim's AddressId.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - These routes run only `isAuthorized` (`server.ts:382`, 386, 444), unlike Cards and Addresses, which use `appendUserId`. The models declare a writable UserId (`complaint.ts:28`, `recycle.ts:30`, `privacyRequests.ts:31`). finale create binds the body; the rest of the hook loop was not read.

**Fix:** ◕ [M-331](#m-331) — Set the owner server-side and allow-list create fields

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03)

<a id="t-170"></a><a id="f-170"></a>
#### F-170 · Order ownership bound to non-unique vowel-masked email

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-05](#c-05) - marsdb Embedded Store  ·  **Location:** `data/datacreator.ts:751`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** The order seed stores only `email.replace(/[aeiou]/gi,'*')` as the order owner. Any consumer that authorizes order access by comparing masked emails will treat different users as the same owner. A user who registers a colliding email could read another user's orders or act on them.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - In `datacreator.ts` at lines 751, 760 and 769, `ordersCollection` seed records carry only a lossy masked email as the owner field.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-332](#m-332) — Store unique UserId as order owner

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-863](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/863.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-171"></a><a id="f-171"></a>
#### F-171 · Address update by id lacks ownership check

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:450`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: PUT `/api/Addresss/:id` has only `appendUserId`, but finale hooks for Address in `server.ts` lines 541-570 were not served, so absence of an owner check is unproven.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An authenticated customer sends PUT `/api/Addresss/`&lt;`victimAddressId`&gt; with any body. Only `appendUserId` runs before the finale-generated update. The response returns the updated record.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous - `server.ts:450` registers only `appendUserId` for PUT `/api/Addresss/:id`. The finale resource updates by primary key. No hook compares the stored UserId with the caller in the `server.ts` lines served (lines 542-585 were not served).

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-179](#m-179) — Manual review: verify Address update by id lacks ownership check · ◕ [M-183](#m-183) — Scope address updates to the authenticated owner

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01)

<a id="t-172"></a><a id="f-172"></a>
#### F-172 · Any authenticated user can list all user accounts

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:601`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** A self-registered customer calls GET `/rest/user/authentication-details` with their own JWT. The handler runs `UserModel.findAll` and returns every user's `dataValues: emails`, roles, last login time and other profile fields, with password length shown as asterisks.

There is no administrator role check.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:398` applies only `isAuthorized` (JWT validity) to `/rest/user/authentication-details`. The handler (`routes/authenticatedUsers.ts:12`,25) returns all users with no role or caller scoping.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-333](#m-333) — Restrict the user list endpoint to administrators

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-113"></a><a id="f-113"></a>
#### F-113 · Security answers hashed with a fast HMAC

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Sequelize application data access  ·  **Location:** `models/securityAnswer.ts:45`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-041](#w-041) - Security-sensitive data uses weak cryptographic primitives

**Issue:** An attacker who gets the SecurityAnswers table and the source-embedded HMAC key can brute-force the low-entropy answers offline, then use them to reset other users' passwords. Recovered security answers allow password resets for the affected accounts.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - The answer setter at `models/securityAnswer.ts:45` stores `security.hmac(answer)`. That function is HMAC-SHA256 with a hardcoded key.

**Fix:** Replace the broken hash with a salted password-hashing function (bcrypt/Argon2id) → ◑ [M-277](#m-277) — Hash security answers with a salted adaptive KDF

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-916](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/916.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-082"></a><a id="f-082"></a>
#### F-082 · Open redirect

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/redirect.ts:19`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An attacker sends a victim the link `/redirect?to`=`https://evil.example/?x=https://github.com/juice-shop/juice-shop`. `isRedirectAllowed` only checks whether the URL contains an allowlisted entry, so the check passes and `res.redirect` sends the victim to the attacker's phishing site under the shop's domain.

Phishing and credential theft that borrow the trusted shop domain.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `redirect.ts:15` reads `query.to` and line 16 calls `isRedirectAllowed`, which matches substrings via includes. Line 19 then calls `res.redirect(toUrl)`. The route is unauthenticated.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-248](#m-248) — Validate redirect targets against an allowlist

**Classification:** Open Redirect · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-601](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/601.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-084"></a><a id="f-084"></a>
#### F-084 · OAuth implicit flow without state, nonce or PKCE

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Client Application  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:148`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHN-06](#tc-authn-06), [TC-AUTHN-07](#tc-authn-07))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-024](#w-024) - OAuth sign-in requests are not bound to the user's browser session

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-9](#tb-9) - Angular Client Application → external: The implicit-flow authorize request at this boundary carries no state, so the identity callback cannot be bound to the user's session.

**Issue:** At `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:148` the client starts Google sign-in with `response_type=token` and sends no state parameter. The verification part adds that no nonce or PKCE is sent and that any URL with an #`access_token` fragment is accepted as the callback.

The redirect URI is checked against the configured `authorizedRedirects` allow-list (`login.component.ts:80`).

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `login.component.ts:148` builds the authorize URL with `response_type=token` and no state or nonce. `oauth.component.ts:71-77` parses the fragment without state validation. `user.service.ts:68` sends `access_token` as a query parameter.

**Fix:** Enforce a same-origin or signed CSRF token on every state-changing endpoint → ◑ [M-250](#m-250) — Add anti-CSRF protection to state-changing requests (🟡 [F-084](#f-084))

**Classification:** Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF) · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-06](#tc-authn-06), [TC-AUTHN-07](#tc-authn-07)

<a id="t-089"></a><a id="f-089"></a>
#### F-089 · User username and email persisted with weak

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Sequelize application data access  ·  **Location:** `models/user.ts:49`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** When `persistedXssUserChallenge` is enabled, a registering or profile-editing user can submit an email containing HTML, which is stored unsanitized, or a username crafted to evade the single regex in `sanitizeLegacy`. The value could then execute in an admin or user view.

Stored XSS in user identity fields shown to administrators could lead to session theft and privileged actions.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - At `models/user.ts:48-53`, the username setter uses the regex-based `sanitizeLegacy` when the challenge is enabled. At `models/user.ts:60-70`, the email setter stores raw input in that same branch. The rendering sink was not served.

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-255](#m-255) — Apply sanitizeSecure to username and email always

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-096"></a><a id="f-096"></a>
#### F-096 · Data erasure request without re-verification

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/dataErasure.ts:83`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-025](#w-025) - Cookie-authenticated changes lack request authenticity checks

**Issue:** POST `/dataerasure` creates a deletion PrivacyRequest and clears the session using only the token cookie. It ignores the submitted email and `securityAnswer` and checks no CSRF token. SameSite was not verified.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Enforce a same-origin or signed CSRF token on every state-changing endpoint → ◑ [M-260](#m-260) — Verify security answer and CSRF token before erasure

**Classification:** Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-097"></a><a id="f-097"></a>
#### F-097 · Cross-site request forgery on profile

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/updateUserProfile.ts:38`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-025](#w-025) - Cookie-authenticated changes lack request authenticity checks

**Issue:** POST `/profile` authenticates only by the token cookie and changes the username from `req.body.username`. The handler has no CSRF token or Origin check, and the cookie is set without an explicit `SameSite` attribute (line 42).

Because the username is evaluated and templated (-003/-004), this can also plant a payload.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - No CSRF control exists in the handler. Exploitation depends on the browser's default SameSite behaviour, which the source does not show.

**Fix:** Enforce a same-origin or signed CSRF token on every state-changing endpoint → ◑ [M-261](#m-261) — Add CSRF token and SameSite=Strict session cookie

**Classification:** Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-104"></a><a id="f-104"></a>
#### F-104 · Last login IP from token rendered as trusted

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Client Application  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/last-login-ip/last-login-ip.component.html:10`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** The `lastLoginIp` claim decoded from the JWT is interpolated into HTML, marked as trusted and rendered through [`innerHTML`]. If an attacker can influence the IP value the server records, for example through a client-supplied IP header, the stored markup runs when the user views the Last Login IP page.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-268](#m-268) — Bind the last login IP as text

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04)

<a id="t-105"></a><a id="f-105"></a>
#### F-105 · Stored XSS

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Client Application  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/last-login-ip/last-login-ip.component.ts:39`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An authenticated user sends GET `/rest/saveLoginIp` with a True-Client-IP header that holds HTML or script. When the XSS challenge is enabled, the server stores the value unsanitized. Only the user's own record is written; no cross-user delivery path is shown.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `last-login-ip.component.ts:39` wraps `payload.data.lastLoginIp` in DomSanitizer trust-HTML, and the template binds it with [`innerHTML`] (html line 10). `routes/saveLoginIp.ts:18` reads the true-client-ip header, and line 25 sanitizes it only when the challenge is disabled.

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-269](#m-269) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (🟡 [F-105](#f-105))

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-106"></a><a id="f-106"></a>
#### F-106 · Product descriptions marked trusted

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Client Application  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/product-details/product-details.component.html:16`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** The search view marks every product description as trusted HTML. The product details dialog then renders the description through [`innerHTML`].

Anyone who can change a product description on the server, for example through the product API, gets script running for every shopper who opens that product.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-270](#m-270) — Remove the trust bypass for product descriptions

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04)

<a id="t-107"></a><a id="f-107"></a>
#### F-107 · Angular HTML sanitization disabled for challenge

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Client Application  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/score-board/score-board.component.ts:82`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** The score board passes every challenge description received from the challenges API through DomSanitizer bypass. It then binds the result to [`innerHtml`] in the challenge card. That script can read the JWT kept in `localStorage`.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Line 82 sets description to `this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(challenge.description)`. `challenge-card.component.html` line 9 renders it with [`innerHtml`]. The source of the server-side description and whether an attacker can write it were not shown.

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-271](#m-271) — Sanitize challenge descriptions instead of bypassing trust

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-108"></a><a id="f-108"></a>
#### F-108 · Product descriptions marked as trusted HTML

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Client Application  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:110`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An attacker who can change a product description through the products API stores script markup in it. The search page marks every description as trusted HTML, so the markup runs for every visitor wherever the description is rendered as HTML.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `trustProductDescription` bypasses sanitization for every product description returned by the search API. The rendering component (ProductComponent) and the server write path were not read.

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-272](#m-272) — Sanitize product descriptions instead of trusting them

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-109"></a><a id="f-109"></a>
#### F-109 · Order ID from tracking response rendered

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Client Application  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/track-result/track-result.component.html:9`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** The track-result view takes the id from the URL, sends it to the tracking API, and wraps the returned `orderId` in &lt;code&gt; HTML that it marks as trusted and binds through [`innerHTML`]. If the server echoes the requested id, a crafted tracking link runs script in the victim's browser.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-273](#m-273) — Bind the tracked order number as text

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04)

<a id="t-110"></a><a id="f-110"></a>
#### F-110 · Chat auto-sends attacker-supplied

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Client Application  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/chatbot/chat-conversation/chat-conversation.component.ts:80`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-025](#w-025) - Cookie-authenticated changes lack request authenticity checks

**Issue:** An attacker sends a victim a link such as /#`/chatbot/conversation/x?initialMessage=``&lt;prompt&gt;`. `ngOnInit` reads the `initialMessage` query parameter and calls `sendMessage` with it at once, without the user confirming.

An attacker-chosen LLM prompt runs under the victim's identity and may invoke backend tools.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `chat-conversation.component.ts:77-80` takes `queryParams['initialMessage']` and calls `sendMessage(initialMessage)` without user interaction. The server-side chat tool capabilities were not inspected.

**Fix:** Enforce a same-origin or signed CSRF token on every state-changing endpoint → ◑ [M-274](#m-274) — Add anti-CSRF protection to state-changing requests (🟡 [F-110](#f-110))

**Classification:** Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-091"></a><a id="f-091"></a>
#### F-091 · Published container images lack signature and provenance attestation

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Found by:** survey and scanners

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Instances (2):** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: 1; `.github/workflows/release.yml`: 86

**Issue:** The release and CI docker jobs, at `.github/workflows/release.yml:86` and `.github/workflows/ci.yml:1`, push bkimminich/juice-shop images using a long-lived DockerHub token, without cosign signing or build provenance. Consumers cannot verify where an unsigned image came from, so any registry intermediary could substitute it.

An SBOM is generated inside the image, and the DockerHub credentials are scoped to the docker jobs.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Pin the signature algorithm explicitly and reject `alg:none` and unknown algorithms → ◑ [M-180](#m-180) — Sign and verify release artifacts

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-SUPPLY-04](#tc-supply-04) · Rules: IAC-040

<a id="t-092"></a><a id="f-092"></a>
#### F-092 · Heroku CLI installed by piping unverified remote script to shell

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:358`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** The heroku deploy job pipes `install.sh` from cli-assets.heroku.com straight into sh with no checksum or signature check. If that content is compromised or substituted, attacker code runs in the job that later deploys production with `HEROKU_API_KEY`.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-257](#m-257) — Install Heroku CLI from a pinned, checksum-verified artifact

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-494](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/494.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-093"></a><a id="f-093"></a>
#### F-093 · Untrusted npm Install/Postinstall Scripts Enabled

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-026](#w-026) - Untrusted npm Install/Postinstall Scripts Enabled

**Instances (2):** `Dockerfile`: 4; `package.json`: 56

**Issue:** Dependency Install Scripts Run at Build Time: Without `--ignore-scripts`, any compromised dependency publisher can execute postinstall code at build time.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-162](#m-162) — Install npm dependencies with --ignore-scripts in the `Dockerfile`

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-506](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/506.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-SUPPLY-03](#tc-supply-03) · Rules: IAC-005

<a id="t-094"></a><a id="f-094"></a>
#### F-094 · Container base images referenced by floating tags without digest

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Found by:** survey and scanners

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-042](#w-042) - Build pipeline trusts mutable third-party references

**Instances (2):** `Dockerfile`: 1; `test/smoke/Dockerfile`: 1

**Issue:** At `Dockerfile:1`, the image builds from node:24 and gcr.io/distroless/nodejs24-debian13 without digests. The smoke-test image at `test/smoke/Dockerfile:1` uses a tag-only alpine base. The build is multi-stage, with a distroless runtime and the non-root USER 65532.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-258](#m-258) — Pin base images by sha256 digest

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-SUPPLY-01](#tc-supply-01) · Rules: IAC-001

<a id="t-095"></a><a id="f-095"></a>
#### F-095 · Release job installs global build tools and dependencies

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.github/workflows/release.yml:38`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-042](#w-042) - Build pipeline trusts mutable third-party references

**Issue:** The release package job installs typescript, cyclonedx-npm and `grunt-cli` globally with ranges or no version, then runs npm install `--production` with lifecycle scripts enabled. A malicious new release of any of these tools runs in the job that attaches archives to the GitHub release using `GITHUB_TOKEN`, and can backdoor the published distribution.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-259](#m-259) — Pin exact tool versions and use npm ci

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-1357](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1357.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-150"></a><a id="f-150"></a>
#### F-150 · Workflows rely default permissions

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:24`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** `ci.yml` has no permissions block, and neither do `release.yml`, `image_actions.yml`, `lint-fixer.yml`, `rebase.yml`, `stale.yml`, `zap_scan.yml` and others. Every job inherits the repository's default token scope.

If that default is read-write, any compromised step (mutable action, malicious dependency postinstall) can push code, change releases or modify issues.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-314](#m-314) — Drop unnecessary privileges in build and runtime

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-250](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/250.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-151"></a><a id="f-151"></a>
#### F-151 · Container Runs as Root

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `test/smoke/Dockerfile:1`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** Container Runs as Root: Containers running as root expand container-escape blast radius.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-163](#m-163) — Run the smoke-test image as a non-root user

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-250](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/250.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-INFRA-01](#tc-infra-01) · Rules: IAC-002

<a id="t-152"></a><a id="f-152"></a>
#### F-152 · Missing Workflow Permissions Block

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (13)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-023](#w-023) - Missing Workflow Permissions Block

**Instances (13):** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: 25; `.github/workflows/frontend-bundle-analysis.yml`: 13; `.github/workflows/image_actions.yml`: 21; `.github/workflows/lint-fixer.yml`: 6; `.github/workflows/rebase.yml`: 8; `.github/workflows/release.yml`: 10; `.github/workflows/stale.yml`: 7; `.github/workflows/update-challenges-ebook.yml`: 11 … (+5 more)

**Issue:** Missing Workflow Permissions Block: Without an explicit permissions block at workflow or job level, the job inherits the repository default (commonly write-all) for `GITHUB_TOKEN` - any compromised step can push code, create releases, or approve PRs.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-164](#m-164) — Declare least-privilege GITHUB_TOKEN permissions in workflows

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-732](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/732.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-CI-01](#tc-ci-01) · Rules: IAC-010

_**Reliability** says how sure this report is that a finding is real, not how severe it is. It is a judgement, not a probability._

_⚠ Fully viable: no effective control blocks the chain · ◐ Partially blocked: at least one step has a compensating control · ✓ Mitigated: the chain is broken at a verified step · ? Inconclusive: could not be verified end to end. Steps: ✓ confirmed · ✗ refuted · ? inconclusive._

_Proposed fixes address confirmed steps; their effectiveness still requires validation._

<a id="ac-t-006"></a>
### AC-T-006 — Remote Code Execution via Server-Side Injection

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Execute arbitrary code in the application process.

**Prerequisite:** An input reaches a server-side interpreter / template / eval.

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — Server-side code execution | Attacker-controlled input is passed to `eval`,<br/>a server-side template engine, an unsafe<br/>sandbox, or an unsafe deserializer. |

**Scenario impact**

A single injection into a server-side interpreter yields code execution in the application process - the highest-impact outcome, granting full filesystem and network access from one unauthenticated request.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ● [M-187](#m-187) — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (🔴 [F-011](#f-011)) (**P1**): addresses 🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — Server-side code execution at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ◕ [M-200](#m-200) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (🟠 [F-028](#f-028)) (**P2**): addresses 🟠 [F-028](#f-028) — Basket item update not bound to the caller's basket at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

<a id="ac-t-005"></a>
### AC-T-005 — Authentication Bypass via Exposed Secret Material

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Forge trusted tokens / credentials and impersonate any user.

**Prerequisite:** Signing material or other secrets are reachable (committed to a public repo, served by an unauthenticated route, or in an exposed directory).

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-022](#f-022) — Hardcoded RSA private key used to sign session JWTs | A private key, signing secret, or credential<br/>file is committed to the source repository<br/>or served without authentication. |
| 2 | 🟠 [F-019](#f-019) — JWT verification does not pin the accepted signature | The exposed key/secret is the same one the<br/>server trusts, so a token signed with it (or<br/>the leaked credential) is accepted as<br/>authentic. |

**Scenario impact**

Exposed signing material collapses the entire authentication boundary: any attacker who reads the key can mint a valid token for any identity or role, with no credential ever required.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ● [M-194](#m-194) — Load JWT signing key from a secret store (**P1**): addresses 🟠 [F-022](#f-022) — Hardcoded RSA private key used to sign session JWTs at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.
- ◕ [M-191](#m-191) — Pin RS256 in every token verification call (**P2**): addresses 🟠 [F-019](#f-019) — JWT verification does not pin the accepted signature at **Step 2**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

<a id="ac-t-007"></a>
### AC-T-007 — Privileged Action via Prompt Injection into a Tool-Calling Model

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🟡 Medium · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Make the model invoke a privileged tool or return data the attacker is not entitled to, using the application's own authority.

**Prerequisite:** Attacker can place text into a conversation, document, or record that the application later sends to a language model.

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟡 [F-103](#f-103) — Stored username and reviews enter model context as instructions | The handler builds the prompt by<br/>interpolating request or stored text into<br/>the same string as the system instructions,<br/>so attacker text is read as instruction. |
| 2 | _no matching finding_<br/>`routes/chat.ts:184` | Tools exposed to the model run with the<br/>application's authority; a manipulated model<br/>turn therefore performs actions the caller<br/>could not. |

**Scenario impact**

Text an attacker can place anywhere the application later feeds to the model becomes instruction, and the tools behind that model run with the application's authority rather than the caller's - so a comment or a stored record reaches actions the attacker was never entitled to.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ◑ [M-267](#m-267) — Isolate stored user text from model instructions (**P3**): addresses 🟡 [F-103](#f-103) — Stored username and reviews enter model context as instructions at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

<a id="ac-t-002"></a>
### AC-T-002 — Bulk Data Exfiltration via Broken Object Authorization

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - authenticated low-privilege user · **Combined Risk:** 🟠 High · **Verdict:** ◐ Partially blocked

**Goal:** Enumerate and exfiltrate other users' records, then escalate own permissions via unguarded mass assignment.

**Prerequisite:** Attacker holds a valid, non-privileged user account.

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-002](#f-002) — Basket and basket item endpoints lack ownership checks | Attacker enumerates and retrieves records<br/>for arbitrary object IDs; no ownership<br/>comparison is performed. |
| 2 | 🟠 [F-061](#f-061) — User role attribute assignable | Update endpoint persists an unfiltered `role`<br/>(or equivalent) field supplied in the<br/>request body. |

**Scenario impact**

The ownership gap exposes every record, and the mass-assignment gap lets the same low-privilege actor self-elevate - together they turn a single compromised account into full tenant data access and role escalation.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ◕ [M-182](#m-182) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (🟠 [F-002](#f-002)) (**P2**): addresses 🟠 [F-002](#f-002) — Basket and basket item endpoints lack ownership checks at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

Each mitigation block lists the findings it **Addresses**, the CWEs it **Prevents**, and the **Priority** (P1 = before deployment, P2 = current sprint, P3 = next quarter, P4 = backlog). **Why**, **How**, and **Verification** appear when written; every P1 or P2 mitigation that changes code gives **How** and **Verification**. If a field is omitted, refer to the linked finding's *Evidence* line for file:line context and to the threat-category description in [§8 Findings Register](#8-findings-register) for the underlying weakness.

### P1 — Immediate

<a id="m-181"></a>
#### M-181 — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (F-001)

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-001](#f-001) — Attacker-Controlled Owner ID Authorization Bypass

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/memory.ts:15`

**How:** Replace `req.body.UserId`/`userId`/`ownerId` with `req.user.id` (or equivalent session-derived identity) in every WHERE/filter clause.

1. Add a regression test (or CI check) that fails on the vulnerable pattern and passes once the fix is in place.

_Generic CWE-639 pattern, not code from this repository:_

```typescript
// Ownership check before touching a resource.
const basket = await Basket.findByPk(req.params.id)
if (!basket || basket.UserId !== req.user.id) return res.status(403).end()
```

**Verification:** Re-run the AUTHZ-001 scanner check; it reports no match at `routes/memory.ts:15`, and the regression test passes.

---

<a id="m-184"></a>
#### M-184 — Exchange OAuth tokens server-side instead of derived passwords

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-004](#f-004) — OAuth account password derived from public email address

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1391](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1391.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/oauth/oauth.component.ts:30`

**How:**

1. Remove the client-side password derivation and send the OAuth credential (ID token or authorization code) to a dedicated server endpoint.
2. On the server, verify the Google token (signature, aud, iss, exp) and issue the session from the verified identity.
3. Create OAuth accounts with a random unusable password the client never sees, or disable password login for them.
4. Rotate the passwords of existing OAuth-created accounts.

**Verification:** POST `/rest/user/login` with an OAuth user's email and `btoa(reversed email)` as the password, and confirm the server answers 401.

**Reference:** [CWE-1391](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1391.html)

---

<a id="m-185"></a>
#### M-185 — Use parameterized replacements in search query

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-005](#f-005) — SQL injection in product search query

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/search.ts:23`

**How:**

1. In `routes/search.ts`, replace the template string with a parameterized `sequelize.query` that uses a :q placeholder in the LIKE clause and replacements: { q: `%${criteria}%` }.
2. Or, instead of the raw query, use `ProductModel.findAll` with `Op.like` conditions.
3. Check that q is a string and reject arrays or objects.

_Generic CWE-89 pattern, not code from this repository:_

```typescript
// Reject string interpolation; use parameter binding.
await sequelize.query(
  'SELECT * FROM Users WHERE email = :email AND password = :password',
  { replacements: { email, password: hash(password) }, type: QueryTypes.SELECT }
)
```

**Verification:** Send `/rest/products/search?q=``')) UNION SELECT id,email,password,4,5,6,7,8,9 FROM Users-- and /rest/products/search?q='`)) `UNION SELECT sql,2,3,4,5,6,7,8,9 FROM sqlite_master`--. Confirm that neither response contains user emails or schema rows, and that each returns an empty product list.

**Reference:** [CWE-89: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements used in an SQL Command (SQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html)

---

<a id="m-186"></a>
#### M-186 — Use parameterized queries for login lookup

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-010](#f-010) — SQL injection in login query through the email field

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/login.ts:34`

**How:**

1. Replace the raw template-literal query with `UserModel.findOne({ where: { email, password: security.hash(password), deletedAt: null } })`, or call `sequelize.query` with replacements or bind parameters.
2. Check that email and password are strings before the query, and reject requests where they are not.

_Generic CWE-89 pattern, not code from this repository:_

```typescript
// Reject string interpolation; use parameter binding.
await sequelize.query(
  'SELECT * FROM Users WHERE email = :email AND password = :password',
  { replacements: { email, password: hash(password) }, type: QueryTypes.SELECT }
)
```

**Verification:** Send POST `/rest/user/login` with `{"email":"admin@juice-sh.op'--","password":"x"}`. Assert that the response is 401 Invalid email or password and contains no token, and add this as an automated API test.

**Reference:** [CWE-89: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements used in an SQL Command (SQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html)

---

<a id="m-187"></a>
#### M-187 — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (F-011)

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — Server-side code execution

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-001](#w-001)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-95](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/95.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/userProfile.ts:61`

**How:**

1. Delete the eval branch in `routes/userProfile.ts` and always treat the username as plain text.
2. Validate usernames on write in `updateUserProfile` against a strict, restricted character set and a length limit.

**Verification:** Set the username to #{1+1}, load GET `/profile`, and assert that the literal text #{1+1} is rendered rather than 2, so no expression is evaluated.

**Reference:** [CWE-95](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/95.html)

<a id="m-194"></a>
#### M-194 — Load JWT signing key from a secret store

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-022](#f-022) — Hardcoded RSA private key used to sign session JWTs

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-010](#w-010)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) - Use of Hard-coded Credentials

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:21`

**How:**

1. Remove the PEM private key literal and load the key at runtime from a secret manager or a mounted secret file.
2. Rotate the key pair and invalidate tokens signed with the exposed key.
3. Move the HMAC key on line 42 to the same secret store and rotate the stored security answer hashes.

_Generic CWE-798 pattern, not code from this repository:_

```typescript
// Read secrets from env / vault, not the source.
const hmacKey = process.env.ORDER_HMAC_KEY
if (!hmacKey) throw new Error('ORDER_HMAC_KEY not set')
const sig = createHmac('sha256', hmacKey).update(payload).digest('hex')
```

**Verification:** grep the repository for 'PRIVATE KEY' and for the HMAC literal and expect no hits. A token signed with the old key must be rejected by `isAuthorized()` with 401.

**Reference:** [CWE-798: Use of Hard-coded Credentials](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html)

---

<a id="m-237"></a>
#### M-237 — Strip privileged fields from registration requests

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-071](#f-071) — Self-registration accepts a client-supplied admin role

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:408`

**How:**

1. In the POST `/api/Users` pre-handler, delete role, `deluxeToken`, `totpSecret`, `isActive` and id from `req.body`, or build the create payload from an explicit allow-list.
2. Force role to 'customer' in a `resource.create.write.before` hook so the generated endpoint cannot be bypassed.

_Generic CWE-915 pattern, not code from this repository:_

```typescript
// Whitelist mutable fields server-side; never trust the body.
const ALLOWED = ['email', 'username'] as const
const patch = Object.fromEntries(
  Object.entries(req.body).filter(([k]) => ALLOWED.includes(k as any))
)
await user.update(patch)
```

**Verification:** POST `/api/Users` with `role=admin`, then GET the created user (as admin) or decode the login JWT and assert role === 'customer'.

---

<a id="m-238"></a>
#### M-238 — Strip or server-assign role on user registration

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-072](#f-072) — Self-registration can set the admin role through the request

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:501`

**How:**

1. Add a `resource.create.write.before` hook (or pre-handler) for User that deletes role, `deluxeToken`, `totpSecret` and `isActive` from `req.body`
2. Set role to 'customer' on the server for self-registration and change roles only through an admin-guarded endpoint

_Generic CWE-915 pattern, not code from this repository:_

```typescript
// Whitelist mutable fields server-side; never trust the body.
const ALLOWED = ['email', 'username'] as const
const patch = Object.fromEntries(
  Object.entries(req.body).filter(([k]) => ALLOWED.includes(k as any))
)
await user.update(patch)
```

**Verification:** curl -X POST `/api/Users` -H '`Content-Type`: application/json' -d '{"email":"t@`x.io`","password":"p","passwordRepeat":"p","role":"admin"}' must return `data.role` == 'customer'.

### P2 — This Sprint

<a id="m-160"></a>
#### M-160 — Pin JWT verification to RS256

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-014](#f-014) — JWT verify call missing algorithms allowlist

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) - Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/verify.ts:120`

**How:**

1. In `routes/verify.ts`, pass an options object with algorithms: ['RS256'] to the `jwt.verify` call at the evidenced line.
2. Reject the token and return 401 when verification throws or the header alg differs from `RS256`.
3. Use the RSA public key as the only verification key, never a shared secret string.

_Generic CWE-347 pattern, not code from this repository:_

```typescript
// Always verify on the public key; never trust the unsigned header.
const decoded = jwt.verify(token, publicKey, { algorithms: ['RS256'] })
```

**Verification:** Send a token with alg none and a token `HS256`-signed with the public key as secret. Both must be rejected with 401, while a valid `RS256` token is still accepted.

<a id="m-161"></a>
#### M-161 — Render username as escaped Pug local

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-035](#f-035) — Input compiled as template source

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-018](#w-018)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1336](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1336.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/userProfile.ts:87`

**How:**

1. Compile a static Pug template once, without substituting the username into the template source.
2. Pass the username as a local variable so Pug renders it with escaped interpolation.
3. Remove the eval and string-replace paths for usernames in `routes/userProfile.ts`.

**Verification:** Set the username to a#{7*7}, load GET `/profile` and confirm the literal text is shown escaped and unevaluated, while normal usernames still render.

<a id="m-182"></a>
#### M-182 — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (F-002)

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-002](#f-002) — Basket and basket item endpoints lack ownership checks

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:426`

**How:**

1. In `quantityCheckBeforeBasketItemUpdate`, after loading the item, reject the request with 403 when `item.BasketId` differs from the token's bid.
2. Disallow changing BasketId through PUT `/api/BasketItems/:id`.
3. In the GET `/rest/basket/:id`, POST `/rest/basket/:id/checkout` and PUT `/rest/basket/:id/coupon/:coupon` handlers, reject the request when the path id does not match the authenticated user's basket.

_Generic CWE-639 pattern, not code from this repository:_

```typescript
// Ownership check before touching a resource.
const basket = await Basket.findByPk(req.params.id)
if (!basket || basket.UserId !== req.user.id) return res.status(403).end()
```

**Verification:** As user A, send PUT `/api/BasketItems/`&lt;B's item> with a new quantity, then GET `/rest/basket/`&lt;B's id>, POST `/rest/basket/`&lt;B's id>`/checkout` and PUT `/rest/basket/`&lt;B's id>`/coupon/``&lt;coupon&gt;`. Expect 403 on each, with B's data unchanged.

**Reference:** [CWE-639: Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html)

---

<a id="m-183"></a>
#### M-183 — Scope address updates to the authenticated owner

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-003](#f-003) — Missing ownership check on resource IDs
- 🟡 [F-171](#f-171) — Address update by id lacks ownership check

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:450`

**How:**

1. Add a custom PUT `/api/Addresss/:id` handler that updates only where id and UserId match the authenticated user, or add a finale `update.fetch.before` hook that rejects records whose UserId differs from the token user.
2. In `applyCoupon`, load the basket with where { id, UserId } taken from the authenticated session.
3. Reject the request when the basket does not belong to the caller.
4. Replace the generated PUT with a handler that runs `AddressModel.update(fields, { where: { id: req.params.id, UserId: callerId } })` and returns 404 when no row matches.
5. Do not let the update change UserId; use an explicit allow-list of address fields.

_Generic CWE-639 pattern, not code from this repository:_

```typescript
// Ownership check before touching a resource.
const basket = await Basket.findByPk(req.params.id)
if (!basket || basket.UserId !== req.user.id) return res.status(403).end()
```

**Verification:** As user A, PUT `/api/Addresss/`&lt;B's address id> and apply a coupon to B's basket. Both must return 403 or 404, and B's address and coupon field must be unchanged.

**Reference:** [CWE-639: Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html)

<a id="m-188"></a>
#### M-188 — Verify JWT signature before trusting chat identity

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-013](#f-013) — Chatbot identity taken from an unverified JWT

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) - Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:45`

**How:**

1. In `getUserId`, replace `security.decode` with signature verification against the `RS256` public key, using `security.verify` or `jwt.verify` with algorithms ['RS256'].
2. Return undefined and reject the token when verification fails.
3. Register `security.isAuthorized()` on `/rest/chat`, or at least before the order tools run.
4. Bind tool-level ownership checks to the verified subject, and compare orders against the stored user email or user id instead of a vowel-masked email.

_Generic CWE-347 pattern, not code from this repository:_

```typescript
// Always verify on the public key; never trust the unsigned header.
const decoded = jwt.verify(token, publicKey, { algorithms: ['RS256'] })
```

**Verification:** Send POST `/rest/chat` with an unsigned or re-signed JWT for another user id, such as user 2, and ask for that user's order. The tool must answer 'Customer not authenticated'.

**Reference:** [CWE-347: Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html)

<a id="m-189"></a>
#### M-189 — Require the current password

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-015](#f-015) — Password change skips current password check

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-620](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/620.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/changePassword.ts:39`

**How:**

1. Require current and reject the request when it is missing or does not match the stored hash.
2. Move password change to POST with the passwords in a JSON request body so they stay out of URLs and logs.

**Verification:** Call GET `/rest/user/change-password?new=abc12345`&`repeat=abc12345` with a valid token and no current parameter. Expect 401 and an unchanged password.

<a id="m-190"></a>
#### M-190 — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-016](#f-016) — RSA token signing key is hardcoded in source

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-010](#w-010)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) - Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:54`

**How:**

1. Remove the private key literal from source and load it at startup from a secret manager or a protected file.
2. Generate and rotate to a new key pair, and invalidate every token issued with the old key.

_Generic CWE-321 pattern, not code from this repository:_

```typescript
// Load the RSA private key from an environment variable / KMS — never
// the source tree. Rotate the prior key and revoke outstanding tokens.
const privateKey = process.env.JWT_PRIVATE_KEY
if (!privateKey) throw new Error('JWT_PRIVATE_KEY not set')
const token = jwt.sign(claims, privateKey, { algorithm: 'RS256' })
```

**Verification:** Run grep -r 'PRIVATE KEY' lib/ and confirm it returns nothing. Confirm that a token signed with the old key is rejected with 401.

<a id="m-191"></a>
#### M-191 — Pin RS256 in every token verification call

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-019](#f-019) — JWT verification does not pin the accepted signature

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) - Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:52`

**How:**

1. Pass algorithms: ['RS256'] to `expressJwt` in `isAuthorized`.
2. Pass { algorithms: ['RS256'] } to `jwt.verify` in `updateAuthenticatedUsers`.
3. Replace the `jws.verify` cast with `jwt.verify(token, publicKey, { algorithms: ['RS256'] })`.

_Generic CWE-347 pattern, not code from this repository:_

```typescript
// Always verify on the public key; never trust the unsigned header.
const decoded = jwt.verify(token, publicKey, { algorithms: ['RS256'] })
```

**Verification:** Send an `HS256` token signed with the public key and an alg none token to `/rest/2fa/status` and `/rest/2fa/verify`, and confirm both return 401.

<a id="m-192"></a>
#### M-192 — Replace security-question recovery with a CSPRNG reset token delivered…

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-020](#f-020) — Password reset accepts a security-question answer

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-640](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/640.html) - Weak Password Recovery Mechanism

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:10`

**How:** Replace security-question recovery with a CSPRNG reset token delivered through a verified out-of-band channel; expire it quickly and invalidate it after one use.

1. Add a regression test (or CI check) that fails on the vulnerable pattern and passes once the fix is in place.

**Verification:** Re-run the AUTHN-001 scanner check; it reports no match at `routes/resetPassword.ts:10`, and the regression test passes.

---

<a id="m-193"></a>
#### M-193 — Replace question-based reset with an emailed single-use token

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-021](#f-021) — Password reset relies on a guessable security

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-013](#w-013)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-640](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/640.html) - Weak Password Recovery Mechanism

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:41`

**How:**

1. Require a random, single-use, short-lived reset token sent to the registered email address before setting a new password, instead of relying only on a security question.
2. Rate-limit `/rest/user/login` and `/rest/user/reset-password` with `express-rate-limit`, keyed on `req.ip` under a trusted proxy configuration rather than the raw X-Forwarded-For header, and also keyed on the target email.
3. Add per-account lockout or backoff after repeated failed answers or login attempts.

**Verification:** Send 200 reset requests for one email with wrong answers and rotating X-Forwarded-For values. Confirm that HTTP 429 is returned before the 21st attempt. Confirm that a reset without a valid emailed token fails.

<a id="m-195"></a>
#### M-195 — Sanitize product descriptions in every setter branch

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-023](#f-023) — Product description stored without HTML sanitization

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-007](#w-007)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `models/product.ts:45`

**How:**

1. Always call `security.sanitizeSecure(description)` before `setDataValue`, whatever the challenge state.
2. Encode the output in the rendering layer and do not bind descriptions as trusted HTML.

_Generic CWE-79 pattern, not code from this repository:_

```typescript
// Never call bypassSecurityTrust*; let Angular sanitize.
// template:  <div [innerHTML]="product.description"></div>
// component: no DomSanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(...)
this.product.description = raw  // bound directly; Angular escapes
```

**Verification:** Run a test that updates a product description to '&lt;iframe src="javascript:`alert(1)`"&gt;' and asserts that the stored value contains no iframe tag.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Cross Site Scripting Prevention Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Cross_Site_Scripting_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html)

<a id="m-196"></a>
#### M-196 — Pin image-actions and other actions to commit SHAs

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-024](#f-024) — GitHub Actions referenced by mutable tags or branches

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-042](#w-042)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `.github/workflows/image_actions.yml:33`

**How:**

1. Replace coverallsapp/github-action@v2 with the full 40-character commit SHA of the reviewed release, and keep the tag as a comment.
2. Pin calibreapp/image-actions, actions/checkout and peter-evans/create-pull-request in `image_actions.yml` to full commit SHAs.
3. Replace `github/codeql-action/*`@v3 with a full commit SHA on all three steps.
4. Add a job-level permissions block with contents: read to the coverage-report job.
5. Add a permissions block that grants the `image_actions` job only contents: write and `pull-requests: write`.
6. Enable Dependabot or Renovate for github-actions to propose SHA updates.

**Verification:** Run grep -nE 'uses: [^ ]+@(v[0-9]+|main|master)\b' `.github/workflows/*.yml` and confirm that it prints no line for `ci.yml`, `image_actions.yml` or `codeql-analysis.yml`, and that every codeql-action reference has a 40-hex SHA.

**Reference:** [CWE-829: Inclusion of Functionality from Untrusted Control Sphere](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html)

<a id="m-199"></a>
#### M-199 — Confine extracted entries to the upload directory

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-027](#f-027) — Path traversal in ZIP upload extraction writes outside

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-004](#w-004)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-22](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/22.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/fileUpload.ts:34`

**How:**

1. Resolve each entry as `path.resolve(uploadDir, entry.path)` and reject it unless the result starts with `path.resolve(uploadDir)` + `path.sep`.
2. Write to the resolved path, and reject entries containing '..' segments.
3. Skip symlink entries.
4. Cap total and per-entry extracted bytes.

**Verification:** In a unit test, extract an archive containing the entry '../..`/ftp/legal.md`'. Assert that the entry is rejected, `ftp/legal.md` is unchanged, and no file is created outside uploads/complaints.

**Reference:** [CWE-22: Path Traversal](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/22.html)

<a id="m-200"></a>
#### M-200 — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (F-028)

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-028](#f-028) — Basket item update not bound to the caller's basket

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/basketItems.ts:68`

**How:**

1. In `quantityCheckBeforeBasketItemUpdate`, load the item with where { id, BasketId: `user.bid` }, taking the basket id from the authenticated session.
2. Return 403 before calling `next()` when the item does not belong to the caller's basket.

_Generic CWE-639 pattern, not code from this repository:_

```typescript
// Ownership check before touching a resource.
const basket = await Basket.findByPk(req.params.id)
if (!basket || basket.UserId !== req.user.id) return res.status(403).end()
```

**Verification:** Integration test: user A sends PUT `/api/BasketItems/`&lt;id of user B's item> and expects 403, and B's item quantity stays unchanged.

<a id="m-201"></a>
#### M-201 — Query reviews by numeric product equality

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-031](#f-031) — NoSQL \$where injection

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-008](#w-008)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) - Special-Element Injection in Data Query

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/showProductReviews.ts:36`

**How:**

1. Replace the \$where clause with `reviewsCollection.find({ product: Number(req.params.id) })`.
2. Reject non-numeric (NaN) ids with HTTP 400 before running the query.
3. Remove the global blocking sleep helper.
4. Add rate limiting to the `/rest/products/:id/reviews` route.

_Generic CWE-943 pattern, not code from this repository:_

```javascript
// Reject `$where` and operator keys from user-controlled input.
function safeQuery(filter) {
  for (const k of Object.keys(filter)) {
    if (k.startsWith('$')) throw new Error('operator not allowed')
  }
  return collection.find(filter)
}
```

**Verification:** Request GET `/rest/products/sleep`(2000)`/reviews` and GET `/rest/products/1`||true/reviews. Confirm that each returns 400 immediately, in under 100ms, without running the query.

**Prevents CWEs:**

2. Validate the order id against a strict pattern such as `^[\w-]+$` in every configuration.
3. Stop putting `req.params.id` into an executable predicate in `showProductReviews`. Query by a typed field value instead.

```javascript
// Reject `$where` and operator keys from user-controlled input.
function safeQuery(filter) {
  for (const k of Object.keys(filter)) {
    if (k.startsWith('$')) throw new Error('operator not allowed')
  }
  return collection.find(filter)
}
```

**Verification:** Request `/rest/track-order/`'%20||%20true%20||%20' and confirm that the response contains no orders. Send a similar JavaScript payload as the product review id and confirm that it returns no extra reviews and is not evaluated.

<a id="m-205"></a>

**Addresses:**

- [CWE-1427](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1427.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:206`

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting
